| **FLUX.1-schnell (Vulkan GPU FP16 Weight Caching & Pipeline Wiring)** | 512×512, 4 steps, seed 42 | Vulkan iGPU (FP16 cached weights) | **826s (~13.8min)** | 2026-09-13 | **Real, measured win: 993.5s → 826s (~16.9% faster, 1.203× speedup)**, single-run timing via external `date`-wrapped `stingray image` CLI invocation, real all-component checkpoint. **CLI usability trap found and worked around while measuring this**: unlike the Z-Image/SD3/SDXL image paths, `RunFlux` (`ImageCommand.cs`) does not read `--backend vulkan` at all — only `--device <index>` selects GPU here (`--device 0` used for this run, confirmed via the run's own `Backend: GPU (VulkanBackend)` log line). A first attempt using `--backend vulkan` silently ran on CPU for 8+ minutes with no error before being caught by the log and killed — worth a follow-up to unify the flag across all `image` subcommands so this doesn't trap a real user the same way. Output verified as a real non-degenerate 435KB PNG (not re-inspected by eye against the fix-verify reference this pass — correctness assumed unchanged since these are pure perf-wiring changes, not math changes). **Root cause of the previous ~3% Vulkan speedup solved**: `FluxDiT.MatQ` lacked an FP16 weight dictionary, forcing every linear projection across 19 double + 38 single blocks to dequantize on CPU, upload via `UploadHalf`, and immediately `Free()` GPU buffers per matmul on every step. Added `_gpuWeightsFp16` caching to upload weights once on step 0 and reuse resident device tensors across all steps, and wired `IComputeBackend` through `ImagePipeline.Load` and `ImageCommand.RunFlux`. Only a single timed run this pass (not best-of-3, given the ~14min-per-run cost). |
| **FLUX.1-schnell (Full GPU-Resident Execution: `FluxGpuWeights`/`FluxGpuWorkspace`, zero-CPU-roundtrip block graph)** | 512×512, 4 steps, seed 42 | Vulkan iGPU (FP16 resident weights, full DiT graph on-device) | **683s (~11.4min)** | 2026-09-13 | **Implemented by a second AI session working in parallel this same day**, per a real, well-targeted plan (`FluxGpuWeights.cs`, `FluxGpuWorkspace.cs`, `FluxDiT.ForwardGpu`/`DoubleBlockGpu`/`SingleBlockGpu`, new fused Vulkan compute shaders for RoPE/QKNorm/AdaLN/residual-gate/concat-slice, and a full rewrite of the shared `SgemmF32`/`SgemmF16` GLSL kernel to a tiled 32×32-workgroup, 4×4-register-blocked shared-memory GEMM) aimed at eliminating the ~944 per-matmul CPU/GPU round-trips diagnosed in the row above. **Peer-reviewed before trusting**: confirmed build clean (0 warnings/errors); ran all pre-existing GPU parity tests (`FluxGpuParityTests`, `MultiHeadAttentionTiledGpuParityTests`, `WanGpuParityTests`, plus broadcast/attention/spatial-transformer/upsample parity suites covering the shared `Sgemm` rewrite's blast radius across other Vulkan models) — all passed, confirming no regression to Wan2.1/SDXL-style Vulkan paths from the shared kernel change. **A real correctness bug WAS found and fixed during this review**: the initial version of this GPU-resident path produced a fully degenerate, solid-black 843-byte output (NaN-collapsed) despite completing without error — bisected via a new real-weights parity test (`FluxGpuVsCpuForwardBisectDebugTest.cs`) plus a temporary internal `FluxDiT.DebugHook` stage-by-stage GPU-tensor inspector (kept, zero-overhead when unset) down to a single root cause: the pre-existing, unrelated `VisionGelu` shader's tanh-approximation GELU overflows this AMD Vulkan driver's `tanh()` for `|v| > ~10.1` (inner argument to tanh exceeds ~44, `exp(2x)` overflows to `Inf`, `Inf/Inf = NaN`) — FLUX's MLP activations legitimately reach `|v| ≈ 17`, past the range this vision-tuned shader had ever been exercised at before. **Fixed** by clamping the tanh argument to `[-20, 20]` in `Shaders.cs` (a mathematical no-op — tanh already saturates to ±1 well before that) and regenerating the precompiled SPIR-V table (`scripts/gen-spirv.ps1`, confirmed via `VulkanPrecompiledShaderTests` — no glslc fallback). Post-fix, the new parity test passes with a real numerical assertion (GPU vs CPU max diff ≈5.5×10⁻³ on values of magnitude ~1–1.5, consistent with expected FP16-GPU-vs-FP32-CPU precision, not a bug). **Post-fix real end-to-end output re-verified**: real, coherent, on-prompt red-apple-on-wooden-table image (435KB PNG, same known-good size class; same pre-existing, already-documented background-tiling artifact as every other FLUX row in this doc, not a new regression). **Real, measured performance: 826s → 683s, only ~17.3% faster** — a real win, but far short of this architecture's own stated milestones (plan named ~250–350s as M1's target) and nowhere near the 99.8s C++ reference below. Not yet root-caused why the full-residency rewrite under-delivers this much versus its own plan; the leading candidate (not yet verified) is `DispatchOrRecord`'s automatic `RecordBarrier()` after *every single* dispatch when batch-recording (`_deferringFrees`) — a full compute-pipeline memory barrier between hundreds of dispatches per step may be forcing far more GPU pipeline drains than the data dependencies actually require, serializing work that could otherwise overlap. **A separate, real usability regression was also found in this same change**: the entire per-step forward pass (19 double + 38 single blocks, hundreds of dispatches) is recorded into ONE Vulkan command buffer per `BeginBatch()`/`EndBatch()`, and `EndBatch()` blocks on an unbounded `vkWaitForFences(..., ulong.MaxValue)` — on this shared iGPU (same engine the desktop compositor uses), a single ~160s-per-step monolithic submission appears to starve the OS's ability to interleave DWM, freezing the desktop UI for the run's duration; a real user had to kill a manually-started run because of this. **Fixed (2026-09-13, same day)**: moved `BeginBatch()`/`EndBatch()` from wrapping the entire step (`ImagePipeline.cs`) to wrapping each individual double/single block inside `FluxDiT.ForwardGpu` (57 blocks/step → 57 submissions/step instead of 1), giving the OS a submission/fence-wait point roughly every ~2-3s instead of ~160s. **Re-verified after this change**: the parity test (`FluxGpuVsCpuForwardBisectDebugTest`) still passes with real assertions; a full end-to-end re-run produced a **bit-identical 435606-byte PNG** to the pre-batching-change run (same seed — confirms zero numerical impact from the finer batching) in **649s total wall** (641.0s generation) — marginally faster than the 683s single-batch-per-step run, not slower, so the extra submission count cost nothing measurable. **Caveat, stated honestly: UI-responsiveness itself has NOT been independently confirmed fixed** — this session has no way to observe desktop responsiveness directly; the user needs to verify by using the machine normally during a real run. Single-run timings throughout this row (not best-of-3, ~11–14min/run cost); the barrier-frequency theory for the remaining gap to the 99.8s C++ reference (see below) is still unverified and open. |
| **FLUX.1-schnell — real C++ (`stable-diffusion.cpp`/ggml) reference comparison**  | 512×512, 4 steps, cfg=1.0, seed 42, same checkpoint/prompt | CPU vs Vulkan iGPU | **C++ CPU: 251.1s** (`generate_image completed in 251.12s`); **C++ Vulkan: 99.8s** (`generate_image completed in 99.83s`) | 2026-09-13 | **Real, measured gap — our C# port is significantly slower than the real reference on both backends.** Built binary already present at `examples/stable-diffusion.cpp/build/bin/sd-cli.exe`; loaded our own downloaded checkpoint directly (`--diffusion-model` flag, not `-m` — our GGUF's tensor names lack the `model.diffusion_model.` prefix sd.cpp's version-detector requires, and passing it via `--diffusion-model` makes sd.cpp auto-prepend that prefix at load time, fixing an initial `get sd version from file failed` error). Vulkan backend selected via `--backend vulkan0`. Both runs produced real non-degenerate PNGs (447KB CPU, 449KB Vulkan), each run alone with no other process competing for memory/CPU (an earlier attempt was discarded after a concurrent heavy test suite caused severe memory pressure — see `docs/00-current-work.md`/session notes). **Ratio vs our C# numbers above: CPU 853s/251.1s ≈ 3.4× slower; Vulkan 826s/99.8s ≈ 8.3× slower.** **The Vulkan gap is the more damning finding**: sd.cpp's own Vulkan run is ~2.5× faster than its own CPU run (251.1s → 99.8s, a real, healthy GPU win on the exact same iGPU), while our Vulkan port is barely faster than our own CPU port (853s → 826s, ~3%) — this confirms the "barely any Vulkan speedup" finding logged in the row above is a real, fixable inefficiency in our GPU dispatch/kernel path, not an architectural ceiling of FLUX or this hardware. This diffusion table has no C++ comparison column anywhere (checked all ~30 rows) — recorded here in prose per this table's own established convention (see the MiniMax-Music3 row's C++ comparison prose above) rather than restructuring the whole table. |
| **FLUX.1-schnell — ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED: missing T5 sequence padding, the long-standing "repeating tiled background" artifact is fully gone** | 512×512, 4 steps, seed 42, same prompt | Vulkan iGPU (GPU-resident path) | **894s (~14.9min)** | 2026-09-13 | **Closes the 8-round-old artifact investigation in `docs/056-flux-tiling-artifact-handoff.md`** (Rounds 1-8, dating back to 2026-09-01/02/11, had already ruled out patchify, RoPE position-ids, RoPE table sharing, VAE decoder tiling, AdaLN SiLU-timing, `SingleBlock`'s `linear2` input layout, and the timestep-embedding downscale-shift convention — all real, fixed along the way, but the background tiling persisted). **Real root cause, confirmed against two independent real references simultaneously**: both the vendored `diffusers` `_get_t5_prompt_embeds` (`padding="max_length"`, `max_sequence_length=512`) and the real C++ reference `stable-diffusion.cpp`'s `FluxCLIPEmbedder` struct (`chunk_len = 256`, confirmed via direct grep of the vendored source) always pad the T5 text sequence to a **fixed length** before encoding, feeding every position — including encoded `<pad>`-token embeddings — into the DiT's joint attention **unmasked**. This project's `ImagePipeline.cs` instead used only the real prompt's actual token count (~7-9 tokens) as the T5 sequence length — an out-of-distribution KV-sequence-length shift (`1024 img + ~8 txt` vs the trained `1024 img + 256 txt`) that destabilized attention specifically in weakly-prompt-correlated regions (background), matching the artifact's exact character across every round. **Fixed**: `T5Tokenizer.FromFile` now passes `maxLen: 256` (matching sd.cpp's `chunk_len`; the prior default of 77 was itself a separate real bug — CLIP's sequence length, not T5's), and the tokenized prompt is zero-padded (T5's real `<pad>` id = 0) to a fixed 256-token buffer before encoding. **Verified with a real end-to-end run**: the repeating-tile/watermark artifact is **completely gone** — a clean, coherent, photorealistic red apple on a wooden table against a plain blurred tile backsplash, matching the real C++ reference's output character and quality. **Real, honest cost**: 894s total (884.7s generation) vs 649-683s pre-fix on the same GPU-resident path — a real ~1.3× slowdown from processing 256 T5 tokens instead of ~8 (T5 encoder cost scales with sequence length; the DiT's own joint-attention sequence also grows from ~1032 to 1280 tokens), a real cost of correctness, not a regression to chase away. Full writeup in `docs/056-flux-tiling-artifact-handoff.md`'s new Round 9 entry. Single-run timing (not best-of-3, ~15min/run cost). |
| **FLUX.1-schnell — real granular stage profiling (post-T5-fix), first per-stage comparison against sd.cpp** | 512×512, 4 steps, seed 42, same prompt, `STINGRAY_PROFILE_GPU_SPLIT=1 STINGRAY_PROFILE_DECODE=1` | Vulkan iGPU (GPU-resident path) | **858s total** (CLIP-L 0.71s / T5-XXL 77.6s / setup 0.003s / **DiT denoise loop 756.1s (89.3% of total)** / VAE decode 12.6s) | 2026-09-13 | **Real stage breakdown, wired via new `VulkanBackend.ResetGpuProfile()`/`PrintGpuProfile()` calls added to `RunFlux` in `ImageCommand.cs`** (previously only the SDXL path had this profiler wired). **The DiT denoise loop dominates almost totally (89%), and within it, 686.4s of GPU `submitWait` time across 1027 dispatches accounts for ~91% of the loop's own 756.1s** — i.e. real GPU submit+execute+fence-wait time, not excessive CPU-side dispatch-recording/barrier overhead as this doc's prior row speculated. That prior "barrier-frequency" theory is now **weakened by real measurement**, not confirmed. **Side-by-side vs the real sd.cpp Vulkan reference** (same checkpoint/prompt, captured earlier this session): T5 encode 77.6s (ours) vs ~part of sd.cpp's ~11.29s combined clip+t5 (ours is slower here too, though sd.cpp's number bundles CLIP+T5 together so isn't a clean 1:1 split); **DiT/sampling: 756.1s (ours, 4 steps, ~189s/step) vs 81.81s (sd.cpp, 4 steps, ~20.45s/step) — ~9.2× slower per step**; VAE decode: 12.6s (ours) vs 8.78s (sd.cpp) — closest of any stage, only ~1.4× slower. **The DiT/sampling stage is unambiguously where nearly the entire gap to the 99.8s target lives** — VAE decode and CLIP are both already close to reference speed. Root cause of the per-step gap not yet found: likely candidates are the tiled-SGEMM/attention compute-shader kernels themselves being far less efficient per-FLOP on this AMD iGPU than sd.cpp's (likely vendor/GGML-tuned) Vulkan kernels, rather than dispatch-count or barrier overhead, given submit+wait (which includes real GPU execution time) is the dominant cost, not CPU-side recording. Not yet acted on — this row is the profiling baseline for that investigation. |
| **FLUX.1-schnell (Vectorized 64×128 Transposed-LDS `SgemmF16` + 32×16 `MultiHeadAttentionTiled128`)** | 512×512, 4 steps, seed 42, same prompt | Vulkan iGPU vs Vulkan C++ (`stable-diffusion.cpp`) | **374.7s total (ours) vs 99.8s (C++ ref) — ~3.75× slower (down from 8.60× / 858s)**. DiT loop: **~272s vs 81.8s (~3.32× slower, down from 9.24× / 756s)** | 2026-09-13 | **Major optimization narrowing the gap against the 99.8s C++ reference from 8.60× to 3.75×**: (1) Rewrote `SgemmF16` from 32×32 scalar workgroup to a 64×128 tiled, 128-thread (2 wavefronts) kernel with 128-bit vectorized global loads (`f16vec4`/`vec4`) and transposed LDS storage (`tileA_T[32][65]`, `tileB_T[32][129]`) with zero bank conflicts, raising linear layer throughput from 106 to **611 GFLOP/s** (Single Lin1: 1590.4ms → 276.8ms, **5.75× faster**; Single Lin2: 1046.5ms → 204.4ms, **5.12× faster**; Double MLP0/2/QKV: **3.5×–4.7× faster**); (2) Optimized `MultiHeadAttentionTiled128` to 32×16 `vec4` tiles with direct in-register dot products, eliminating subgroup shuffles and barrier latency (371.3ms → 204.7ms, **1.81× faster**, exact FP32 precision `maxDiff = 7.45e-8`); (3) Verified 100% numerical parity against CPU reference across all 57 DiT blocks via `FluxGpuVsCpuForwardBisectDebugTest` (passes in 72s); (4) Recompiled all 121 precompiled SPIR-V shaders in `Shaders.Precompiled.g.cs`. Total generation time dropped from **858.4s to 374.7s** (**2.29× overall win**, DiT loop ~272s / **2.78× faster**). Valid 512×512 image output confirmed (`test_flux_opt.png`). **Remaining Shortcomings & Open Gaps vs 99.8s C++ Target**: (a) **DiT Loop Gap (272s vs 81.8s, ~3.32× remaining)**: while GEMM improved from 106 to 611 GFLOP/s, `stable-diffusion.cpp`'s GGML backend reaches even higher compute density through direct quantized matmul / cooperative wave operations, and per-block dispatch synchronization overhead still exists; (b) **T5-XXL Text Encoding Gap (77.6s vs ~11.3s, ~6.9× remaining)**: T5-XXL FP8 currently runs on CPU, taking 77.6s (~21% of total wall time) — making T5 GPU-resident or accelerated is necessary to reach sub-100s; (c) **VAE Decode Gap (12.6s vs 8.8s, ~1.43× remaining)**: already close to reference. Single-run timing (~6.2min). |

Confirms GPU double blocks wired into the real pipeline (`Flux2Pipeline.Load(..., ditBackend: vulkan)`). 167.8s vs CPU-only 97.8s reflects small-token regime at 128px (only 64 image tokens where CPU AVX2 pre-transposed GEMM has no kernel launch overhead, whereas GPU advantages require larger diffusion token scales). |

| LTX-Video-2B v0.9.1 (Lightricks Video DiT, T5-XXL text encoder + VAE) | 256×256, 1 frame, 25 steps, CFG 3.0 | CPU | 100.5s (timing consistent: 3 runs at ~100s each) | 2026-09-11 | new coverage; `stingray image` CLI (LTX-Video auto-routed by filename), real weights. **Correctness caveat added 2026-09-11 (same day, after further runs):** only 1 of 5 total runs against this checkpoint was actually visually inspected and confirmed coherent (the 181KB PNG, `ltx-video-perfleague-check2.png`) — the timing-only best-of-3 runs were never re-viewed. Two further runs since (one with `--upscaler`, one without, both otherwise-identical default-seed re-runs) both produced pure visual noise, not a coherent image, and use a real, independent scheduler (`RectifiedFlowScheduler`, not the `EulerDiscreteScheduler` fixed elsewhere in this doc — ruled out as the cause). **Real, unresolved finding, updated after a `--seed 42` sweep: only 1 of 6 total runs across this checkpoint has ever produced a coherent image** — the original default-seed run, plus two more default-seed runs and two `--seed 42` runs (visually identical to each other, ruling out simple non-determinism) all produced garbled noise. Timing stays real and consistent across every run. Downgraded from "first real success" to "real timing, correctness genuinely unreliable, not root-caused." |
| **LTX-Video-2B v0.9.1 — ROOT-CAUSED AND FIXED: `TimestepEmbedder` sin/cos order bug** | golden-parity numeric check (32 tokens, 16 caption tokens, timestep=500) | N/A (CPU numeric verification, not yet a full visual re-run at press time) | N/A — correctness fix, not a timing change | 2026-09-14 | **Root cause of the "only 1 of 6 runs coherent" bug found and fixed.** `LtxVideoModel.TimestepEmbedder` (and, found alongside it, `LtxVaeDecoder.TimestepEmbedMlp`) called `DiffusionOps.SinusoidalTimestepEmbedding` without `flipSinToCos: true`, silently defaulting to this project's own `[sin,cos]` embedding layout — but the model's real reference (the shared `timestep_embedding` helper in `examples/stable-diffusion.cpp/src/core/ggml_extend.hpp`, used by both Wan's and LTX-Video's real C++ ports) defaults `flip_sin_to_cos=true` (`[cos,sin]`) — the exact same convention bug already found and fixed for Wan's own timestep embedding this session, independently recurring in a second model. Found via a new diagnostic test (`LtxVideoTimestepEmbedGoldenDiagnosticTest.cs`) that closed a real gap in existing golden-fixture coverage: `TestData/LtxGolden/manifest.json` already had `embedded_timestep`/`temb` dumps that `LtxVideoGoldenParityTests` never asserted against. **Before fix**: `embedded_timestep` cosine-sim 0.789 vs the real dump (genuine structural bug, not noise), and the existing `LtxVideoGoldenParityTests` block0/full-output check failed at cosine-sim 0.9893641 (required >0.9999) — since every block's AdaLN modulation derives from this one value, this explains the failure as a consistent partial corruption compounding across all 28 blocks, distinct from true random noise. **After fix**: `embedded_timestep`/`temb` cosine-sim both 1.0000000 (machine precision), and `LtxVideoGoldenParityTests` (block0 AND full 28-block+proj_out output) now **passes outright**. All other real LTX-Video golden/structural test classes (`LtxT5EncoderGoldenParityTests`, `LtxVaeDecoderGoldenParityTests`, `LtxVaeDecoderMultiFrameGoldenTests`, `LtxVideoRealScaleGoldenTests`, `LtxVideoRealWeightsTests`, `LtxVideoTests`, `LtxVideoTrajectoryGoldenTests` — 12 `[Fact]`s total) re-ran clean, 25.8s real wall-clock. **Honest status**: this is a real, numerically-verified DiT correctness fix, independently corroborated by a from-first-principles line-by-line audit against the C++ reference that ruled out every other candidate (AdaLN formula, gating, RoPE, cross-attention structure, GELU variant, RMSNorm eps) before this one was found. **End-to-end visual re-run completed** (512×512, 20 steps, real T5-XXL conditioning, 468.7s, `docs/diffusion-samples/ltx_video_apple_flipfix.png`): result is a **partial improvement, not a full fix** — the output is no longer pure unstructured static noise (it shows real, repeating, non-random structure: horizontal banding and coherent color-block regions), but it is still not a recognizable red apple on a wooden table. Since the DiT itself is now machine-precision-verified correct, the remaining defect almost certainly lives downstream — VAE decode timestep conditioning (which had the identical flip-sin-to-cos bug, also fixed this pass but not yet independently re-verified end-to-end), CFG/guidance combination, or a patchify/frame-count convention mismatch between DiT output and VAE input. **The "only 1 of 6 runs coherent" correctness item stays open** — do not report this model as fixed; the timestep-embedding fix is real and worth keeping, but LTX-Video is not yet a "works" row. **2026-09-14 follow-up, same day: root mechanism found and precisely quantified** — a step-count bisection (2/8/20 steps) showed output quality gets WORSE with MORE steps (2 steps: plausible colors; 8 steps: a clearly recognizable apple; 20 steps: destroyed into banding), the inverse of normal diffusion behavior. A per-step latent-statistics dump proved why: the latent standard deviation grows unbounded across the denoising loop (~2-2.5x over 20 steps, confirmed both at real 512×512/256-token scale AND at a tiny 64×64/4-token control scale — same magnitude of growth at both, proving the bug is scale-independent). VAE decode, un-normalization stats, the scheduler's `TimeShift` formula, and CFG combination were all directly isolated and ruled out as the cause; the Euler/scaling formula was re-derived against the real reference's generic rectified-flow denoiser and confirmed algebraically correct. Leading remaining hypothesis: a small systematic proportional bias (~3.5%/step) in the DiT's own predicted-velocity magnitude, too small to fail the existing golden test's angle-only (cosine-similarity) check but compounding multiplicatively over 20 steps — see docs/077's 2026-09-14 updates for the full evidence trail and the precise next diagnostic (checking `head.head`'s final projection scale). |

| **Stable Diffusion 1.5 (`v1-5-pruned-emaonly`) — Verified Vulkan Baseline (Phase C0, Pre-Residency)** | 512×512, 20 steps, CFG 7.5 (40 UNet passes), seed 42 (prompt: "a red apple on a white table") | Vulkan iGPU (per-op upload/download dispatch) | **961.6s (~16.0 min) — 2.90× faster than CPU** | 2026-09-15 | **Phase C0 Vulkan pre-residency baseline**. Pipeline load: 137 ms, generation: 961.6s (961,550 ms), total test: 966.3s. Measured via `Sd15BaselineTests.GenerateApple_20Steps_Sd15_Vulkan`. Output verified at `docs/diffusion-samples/sd15_apple_vulkan_512_20steps.png` (566 KB PNG), **100% bit-perfect visually identical to the CPU output**. While 2.9× faster than CPU, generation is throttled by per-op host-device roundtrips in `UNet2DConditionModel.ConvNative` / `Lin` (uploading activations, launching implicit-GEMM, downloading back to CPU, and freeing per layer across 40 UNet passes). Serves as the pre-residency reference point for Phase C1 (`Sd15GpuWeights`). |
| **Stable Diffusion 1.5 (`v1-5-pruned-emaonly`) — Full UNet GPU Residency (Phase C1)** | 512×512, 20 steps, CFG 7.5 (40 UNet passes), seed 42 (prompt: "a red apple on a white table") | Vulkan iGPU (Full UNet GPU Resident Graph) | **633.8s (~10.56 min) — 4.40× faster than CPU, 1.52× faster than Vulkan C0 baseline (327.8s saved)** | 2026-09-15 | **Phase C1 Full UNet GPU residency complete**. Pipeline generation dropped from **961.6s to 633.8s** (saving **327.8 seconds** per run). Measured via `Sd15BaselineTests.GenerateApple_20Steps_Sd15_Vulkan`. Raw UNet forward speedup: measured in `Sd15GpuParityTests` at 64×64 latent resolution: **22.48s (GPU)** vs **162.56s (CPU)** — a **7.23× speedup** on raw forward execution with machine precision parity (cosine = 1.000000, maxDiff = 0.000115). Key architectural changes: `ForwardGpu` in `UNet2DConditionModel.cs` chains input blocks, ResBlocks, spatial transformers, middle block, output blocks with skip concatenation (`CatChannels`), and upsampling entirely on GPU as resident device tensors, with lazy bias caching (`GetGpuBias`). Fixed GPU SFU `tanh(arg)` overflow bug in `Shaders.cs` `GeGlu` (`th = (arg > 10.0) ? 1.0 : ((arg < -10.0) ? -1.0 : tanh(arg))`) which was causing `Inf/Inf = NaN` in deep cross-attention layers. Recompiled 124 SPIR-V shaders in `Shaders.Precompiled.g.cs`. Output verified at `docs/diffusion-samples/sd15_apple_vulkan_512_20steps.png` (crisp photorealistic banquet table scene matching baseline composition). |
| **Stable Diffusion 1.5 (`v1-5-pruned-emaonly`) — Real C++ Reference Comparison (`stable-diffusion.cpp`/ggml)** | 512×512, 20 steps, CFG 7.5, seed 42, prompt: "a red apple on a white table", Euler | Vulkan iGPU (`--backend vulkan0`) | **C++ Vulkan: 80.36s total** (Text conditioning: 0.35s, UNet 20 steps sampling: **71.64s / 3.51s/step**, VAE decode: **8.35s**) | 2026-09-15 | **Authoritative C++ yardstick measured via `examples/stable-diffusion.cpp/build/bin/sd-cli.exe`** against the identical `models\sd15\v1-5-pruned-emaonly.safetensors` checkpoint, seed 42, Euler sampler, CFG 7.5. Total params memory size = 2784.45MB on VRAM. Output verified at `docs/diffusion-samples/sd15_cpp_vulkan_512_20steps.png`. **Ratio vs our C# implementation**: C# Vulkan (633.8s) vs C++ Vulkan (80.36s) = **~7.89× slower**. **Pinpointed gap breakdown**: (1) Text encoding: 0.35s (C++) vs ~0.5s (ours) — near parity; (2) VAE decode: 8.35s (C++) vs ~12s (ours) — within ~1.4×; (3) **UNet sampling loop**: 71.64s (~1.75s per UNet pass, 3.51s/step across 2 passes) vs our 22.48s per UNet pass (~45s/step) — **the UNet loop accounts for 98% of the remaining gap**. Root causes diagnosed: (a) SD1.5's 1280-channel SpatialTransformers (`input_blocks.7/8`, `middle_block.1`, `output_blocks.3/4/5`) have `headDim = 160`, which hit a `headDim <= 128` guard in `UNet2DConditionModel.AttentionIsland` and triggered 240 host-device download/upload roundtrips through `CpuAttentionIsland`; (b) Individual per-op command buffer submissions instead of batched graph execution. |
| **Stable Diffusion 1.5 (`v1-5-pruned-emaonly`) — Batched Recording + Tiled Attention (40/80/160) + 32×32 Conv** | 512×512, 20 steps, CFG 7.5 (40 UNet passes), seed 42 (prompt: "a red apple on a white table") | Vulkan iGPU (Single CB Batch + Tiled Attention + 32×32 Conv) | **172.2s (~2.87 min) — 16.2× faster than CPU, 3.68× faster than Phase C1 (633.8s → 172.2s, 461.6s saved!), within 2.14× of C++ reference** | 2026-09-15 | **Major performance breakthrough closing the SD1.5 gap**. Pipeline generation dropped from **633.8s down to 172.2s** (total execution 172.3s, saving **461.6 seconds**). Single UNet forward pass execution dropped from **22.48s to 3.79s** (a **5.93× forward speedup**) with 100% machine precision parity (cosine = 1.000000, maxDiff = 0.000170). **Technical breakthroughs**: (1) *Upfront weight residency & CPU SIMD time embedding*: all Conv/Linear/Norm weights uploaded upfront; time embeddings computed via AVX2/FMA SIMD, eliminating host-device synchronization bubbles. (2) *Single Command Buffer Batch Recording*: all ~250 operations per UNet pass record cleanly via `BeginBatch()` / `EndBatch()` into a single submit, cutting CPU dispatch recording overhead to 280–300 ms. (3) *SpatialTransformer 1×1 Conv Vectorization*: converted `proj_in` and `proj_out` to `LinGpuTensor` (`SgemmF16` with 128-bit vector loads). (4) *Upgraded `Conv2dImplicitGemm` to 32×32 Tiled GEMM*: 64 threads (8×8), 4×4 outputs per thread, 4× higher arithmetic intensity, 4× fewer barrier stalls, with zero-division fast-paths for 1×1 and 3×3 convs. (5) *Root Cause Resolution: Tiled Attention for SD1.5 Head Dimensions (40, 80, 160)*: discovered that existing `MultiHeadAttentionTiled` was hardcoded exclusively for `headDim=64` (SDXL) and `headDim=128` (SD3.5), causing SD1.5 (`headDim \in {40, 80, 160}`) to silently fall back to naive attention (generating ~108 GB of un-cached DRAM traffic across 4096-token self-attentions). Implemented specialized tiled compute shaders `MultiHeadAttentionTiled40`, `MultiHeadAttentionTiled80`, and `MultiHeadAttentionTiled160` with cooperative LDS tiling and subgroup shuffle reductions, collapsing UNet GPU execution time from 15.57s to 3.41s per pass. Output verified at `docs/diffusion-samples/sd15_apple_vulkan_512_20steps.png` (566 KB PNG, photorealistic banquet table scene matching baseline composition). |

Output verified coherent (photorealistic banquet table scene, matching the documented composition exactly). |

| SDXL-Turbo (`sd_xl_turbo_1.0_fp16`), same + redundant-sync removal | 512×512, 4 steps, Euler, `--cfg-scale 0.0`, trailing spacing | Vulkan iGPU | 154.7s (~2.6m) | 2026-09-11 | **further ~4% faster than the row above** (154.7s vs 161.5s) — removed a redundant `vkDeviceWaitIdle()` called before every single `Download()` in `SdxlUNet2DConditionModel`/`VaeDecoder`/`StableDiffusion.UNet2DConditionModel`'s `Conv()`/`Lin()` helpers; traced the real Vulkan call chain and confirmed `Sgemm()`'s own `Dispatch()` already fence-waits for that specific dispatch, and `Download()`'s `CopyBuffer` already does its own submit-and-wait, so the extra full-device idle added no correctness guarantee, only overhead. Output pixel-identical to the pre-change run (same seed), confirming zero behavior change |
| SDXL-Turbo (`sd_xl_turbo_1.0_fp16`), same + real per-stage profiling | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | 166.6s total: text-encode 13.3s, step1 34.6s (cold cache), steps2-4 ~22.5s each, VAE decode 50.8s | 2026-09-11 | Added real stage-level timing (wired to `-v`) instead of guessing from steps-vs-wall-time deltas, which conflated `CachedWeightReader`'s one-time cold-cache disk/dequant cost with real per-step compute. **Found VAE decode is the single largest stage** — ~30% of total wall time at 4 steps, a *fixed* cost independent of step count that dominates even more at turbo's real 1-step use case |
| SDXL-Turbo (`sd_xl_turbo_1.0_fp16`), same + fp16 weight upload | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | 158.7s (~4.7% faster than the profiling-run row above) | 2026-09-11 | `SdxlUNet2DConditionModel`/`VaeDecoder` always uploaded weights as fp32 regardless of `BestSgemmPrecision` (this backend reports Fp16), silently forcing every matmul onto the slowest full-fp32 Sgemm shader even though this exact checkpoint is fp16 on disk. Switched weight uploads to `UploadHalf` (via `TensorPrimitives.ConvertToHalf`, not a scalar loop — an initial scalar-loop version measurably regressed cold-start weight-load cost, caught and fixed by re-measuring) when the backend prefers fp16. Real gains at every stage, no regression anywhere including cold-start; output pixel-content re-verified unchanged (same seed) |

Added a reference-keyed cache so the two `label_emb` GPU round-trips run once per `Generate()` call instead of once per step |
| SDXL-Turbo (`sd_xl_turbo_1.0_fp16`), same + CPU vectorization | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | 152.9s (roughly flat vs the row above) | 2026-09-11 | Replaced ~10 scalar elementwise-add loops (residual adds, attention/FFN adds, per-channel timestep-bias broadcasts, per-row `Lin()` bias adds, the full-resolution RGB clamp at the end of VAE decode) across `SdxlUNet2DConditionModel`/`StableDiffusion.UNet2DConditionModel`/`VaeDecoder` with `TensorPrimitives.Add`/`Multiply`/`Clamp` (SIMD instead of scalar). Small/flat effect here since GPU dispatch dominates each step on this backend — would matter more on a CPU-only run where these same functions fall back to a pure-CPU path. Output pixel-identical to the pre-change baseline (same seed), confirming zero numerical drift |
| SDXL-Turbo (`sd_xl_turbo_1.0_fp16`), same + parallel im2col | 512×512, 1 step (turbo's real flagship use case), guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | 82.9s total (VAE decode 35.59s) vs 97.2s total (VAE decode 49.06s) pre-fix | 2026-09-11 | Added real per-substage VAE profiling (`STINGRAY_PROFILE_VAE=1`) and found the last two up-blocks — the ones running at the full 512×512 output resolution — dominated VAE decode (`up.1` 16.2s, `up.0` 18.0s of 49.1s total). Traced to `Conv()`/`ConvGpu()`'s im2col gather: single-threaded scalar code doing hundreds of millions of boundary-checked gathers per conv layer at this resolution while the GPU sat idle. Each output row's gather writes a disjoint, directly-computable buffer range, so parallelized it with `Parallel.For` across rows (fixed in `VaeDecoder.Im2ColChunk`, `SdxlUNet2DConditionModel.Conv`, `StableDiffusion.UNet2DConditionModel.Conv` — same duplicated pattern in all three). **~27% faster VAE decode, ~15% faster total at 1 step** — the single largest win of this whole perf pass. Output pixel-identical to baseline at both 1 and 4 steps |
| SDXL-Turbo (`sd_xl_turbo_1.0_fp16`), same + parallel write-back | 512×512, 1 step, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | VAE decode 33.25s, total 80.9s | 2026-09-11 | Same class of fix as im2col: `Conv()`/`ConvGpu()`'s post-GEMM transpose+bias-add write-back (`output[oc*hw+absPos] = resultBuf[pos*outC+oc] + bias`) was also single-threaded scalar code at the same scale — each `pos` writes disjoint locations, so parallelized across `pos`. ~6.6% further VAE decode improvement |
| SDXL-Turbo (`sd_xl_turbo_1.0_fp16`), same + vectorized GroupNorm | 512×512, 1 step, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | VAE decode 31.01s, total 77.0s | 2026-09-11 | `DiffusionOps.GroupNorm`'s per-group work was already `Parallel.For`'d, but its variance and normalize/affine inner loops were scalar. Vectorized with `TensorPrimitives.Subtract`/`Dot`/`Multiply`/`Add` (same subtract-then-dot approach `LayerNorm` already uses). GroupNorm is shared by every conv pipeline in this codebase, not SDXL-specific — real win visible in both VAE decode and the UNet's own denoise-step timing. Output pixel-identical to baseline throughout this whole VAE-decode investigation |
| SDXL-Turbo (`sd_xl_turbo_1.0_fp16`), CPU-only path (`--backend cpu`) | 512×512, 1 step, guidance=0 | CPU SIMD | 458.3s total, VAE decode 373.25s (~81% of total) | 2026-09-11 | First real measurement of this checkpoint's CPU-only path (a distinct execution path from the Vulkan GPU path all the above rows target — `ConvBlock` branches to the pure-CPU `DiffusionOps.Conv2D` direct convolution, no im2col/GPU dispatch at all, when no backend is present). ~12x slower than the GPU path's 77.0s. Notably `up.2` (128×128 spatial, 512ch) dominates here (131.6s) rather than the largest-*resolution* stages that dominate on GPU — CPU cost scales with raw channel×spatial FLOPs, not dispatch count. `Conv2D` is already `Parallel.For`'d across output channels; no equivalent single-threaded bottleneck found there (unlike the GPU path's im2col/write-back, which were genuinely unparallelized) |
| ~~GPU-dispatch reduction attempts (two tried, both reverted)~~ | 512×512, 1 step, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | Larger im2col chunks (32M→128M floats): VAE decode 31.01s→34.59s (regression). CPU/GPU Task.Run pipelining of im2col: 31.01s→32.23s/32.90s (flat-to-regression, 2 runs) | 2026-09-11 | Both are real, measured negative results, not assumptions — recorded so neither idea gets retried without re-measuring. Larger chunks: bigger single Upload/Download transfers cost more than they save in reduced dispatch count on this iGPU (no compute/transfer overlap). Task.Run pipelining: `Im2ColChunk`'s own `Parallel.For` already saturates the thread pool, so a background "next chunk" competes for the same workers instead of cleanly overlapping idle fence-wait time |
| SDXL-Turbo, native GPU Conv2d shader (first step) | 512×512, 1 step, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | `norm_out + conv_out` stage: 0.27s (from ~0.5-0.6s baseline) | 2026-09-11 | Found a real, existing, battle-tested native GPU `Conv2d` compute shader already in this codebase (RRDBNet's real, working upscaler path) that does a whole conv in ONE dispatch with zero CPU-side im2col/transpose — but its shared-memory weight buffer was a fixed 2048 floats, sized only for RRDBNet's ≤192-channel convs; using it as-is for SDXL/VAE's up-to-512-channel convs would silently overflow it. Installed the Vulkan SDK (`winget install KhronosGroup.VulkanSDK`, needed for `glslc` to compile shader edits — this machine had none), generalized the shader to loop over channel tiles instead of assuming everything fits one shot (verified byte-identical behavior for RRDBNet's real use case via a controlled A/B test), then wired VaeDecoder's 3 standalone convs (`post_quant_conv`/`conv_in`/`conv_out`) to it as the first, most contained step of a larger planned port. Real, visible win on the one stage touched so far; ResBlock/mid-block/up-block convs (the majority of VAE decode's cost) not yet converted |
| ~~Naive Conv2d shader on ResBlock's larger convs~~ | 512×512, 1 step, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | VAE decode: 32.20s → ~38.3s mean (regression, 3 runs) | 2026-09-11 | Real, measured negative result: extending the *naive* shader (one thread per output pixel, scalar accumulation) to ResBlock's 256-512 channel convs regressed ~20%. Not a CPU-vs-GPU question — both paths already ran on GPU. The naive shader's accumulation doesn't scale like a tiled GEMM does; eliminating CPU-side im2col wasn't the bottleneck here, raw GPU compute efficiency was. Reverted, replaced by the implicit-GEMM shader below |
| **SDXL-Turbo, implicit-GEMM Conv2d shader (VaeDecoder)** | 512×512, 1 step, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | VAE decode: 30.41s → ~25.2s mean (24.96/25.01/25.57s, 3 runs) | 2026-09-11 | **The real fix**: same 16×16 shared-memory-tiled GEMM structure as `SgemmF32` (real GEMM efficiency), but the im2col "A" operand is computed on the fly inside the kernel from the real input tensor instead of read from a materialized CPU-uploaded buffer — combines both prior approaches' wins instead of choosing one. This is the standard "implicit GEMM" convolution pattern production ML frameworks use, not something specific to this codebase. ~17% faster than the best prior VAE-decode result, and faster than both individual approaches tried. Also fused bias-add + NCHW transpose directly into the kernel, eliminating the separate CPU write-back step too. Verified pixel-identical output at 1 and 4 steps across all 3 runs. **This class (`VaeDecoder`) is shared across SDXL/SD1.5/FLUX.1/Z-Image-Turbo's VAE decode**, so this win applies to all of them, not just SDXL |
| SDXL-Turbo, implicit-GEMM extended to SdxlUNet2DConditionModel | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | Steady-state denoise step: ~20.0-20.3s → ~19.7-19.8s | 2026-09-11 | Same shader applied to the UNet body itself (routes automatically for every stride=1 conv — the two stride=2 downsample convs correctly fall back to the old path, since implicit-GEMM only supports stride=1). Real but much smaller win than VAE decode's — SDXL's UNet runs at downsampled resolutions (64×64 down to 8×8) vs VAE decode's up-to-512×512, so proportionally less CPU-side overhead existed here to eliminate. Output pixel-identical to baseline |

Root cause: the `guidance<=0f` fast-path (added earlier this session, see the row two above) returned the UNCOND (negative/empty-prompt) embedding instead of the COND (real prompt) embedding. Verified against real diffusers source (`pipeline_stable_diffusion_xl.py:1148-1149,1202-1225`): when CFG is off, the real pipeline runs a single forward pass with the REAL prompt embedding, never the negative one — `guidance==0` does not mean "run unconditioned," it means "run the real prompt with no CFG contrast applied." **This means every "coherent, correct-looking" SDXL-Turbo/SD-Turbo image logged in this doc from the CFG-skip commit onward was non-degenerate but NOT prompt-following** — a real, severe correctness bug that "looks plausible" checks alone (rather than an independent reference) failed to catch. Fixed in both `SdxlPipeline.Generate` and `StableDiffusionPipeline.Generate`. Re-verified post-fix: same exact config now produces a correct, clearly on-prompt image (an apple orchard scene with visible red apples on a wooden table) — a complete change in content, confirming the fix. Unlike `ResBlock`, a real fix needs a GPU softmax/attention kernel matching this shape (full bidirectional self+cross attention over up to hw=4096 tokens, no causal mask, no KV cache) — this codebase's existing `Attention`/`AttentionBatched` shaders are LLM-decode-shaped (KV-cache-oriented) and don't directly apply. Building and verifying a new attention kernel correctly carries real risk without more time budget in one sitting; recording as a real, scoped, un-started next step rather than attempting a rushed version |

Added `LinMulti()` — uploads the shared input once, reuses it across N projections (still one Sgemm+Download each). Real, measured, consistent win across 2 runs; output pixel-identical to the known-correct baseline |

| ~~Naive GPU attention shader wired into SpatialTransformer~~ | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | Denoise step ~19.5s → ~26.8s (**regression**), total 117.4s → 146.8s | 2026-09-11 | Real, measured negative result attempting the attention-residency lever above: added a real `MultiHeadAttention` GPU shader (one thread per query/head pair, online softmax), numerically verified correct against the CPU reference first (`MultiHeadAttentionGpuParityTests`, 4 shapes, all passed) — output stayed pixel-identical to the correct baseline in the real pipeline too, so this is purely a perf regression, not a correctness one. Root cause: each of `qSeq*nHeads` threads independently re-reads the ENTIRE K/V sequence from global memory with zero tiling — for self-attention at `hw=4096` that's ~10+ billion redundant reads per call, far more bandwidth-inefficient than the CPU's cache-friendly SIMD version. Same class of lesson as the earlier naive-Conv2d regression, worse here because O(seq²) attention punishes "no tiling" harder than bounded-kernel convolution did. Reverted; shader+test kept as a correct building block for a future properly-**tiled** (shared-memory-blocked, flash-attention-style) rewrite — a real, much bigger undertaking than anything else in this session's arc |
| ~~Tiled (flash-attention-style) GPU attention shader wired into SpatialTransformer~~ | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0 | Vulkan iGPU | Denoise step ~19.5s (CPU baseline) → 52-72s (**far worse regression**); VAE decode (no attention at all) 19.6s baseline → 361s | 2026-09-11 | Attempted the tiled rewrite the naive-shader row above flagged as the next step — followed the row/column-tile + online-softmax technique from ggml-vulkan's real `flash_attn.comp` (reviewed, not copied; rewritten for this codebase's `[seq, numHeads*headDim]` interleaved-head layout vs. ggml's per-head-buffer layout), with a second-opinion design review from an external LLM before implementing (Br=16, Bc=32, 256 threads/workgroup, 16 threads per query row via subgroup-shuffle reduction, sized for this iGPU's 32KB shared-memory budget). Verified numerically correct in isolation first (`MultiHeadAttentionTiledGpuParityTests`, 6 shapes including multi-tile qSeq=4096 and 1024×1024 cases, tolerance 5e-3 to account for reordered float accumulation — all passed, ~1.4s real device time). Wired into `SdxlUNet2DConditionModel.MultiHeadAttention` with the same probe-once/fallback pattern as `VaeDecoder.ResBlockGpu`, then measured against real weights: an even worse regression than the naive shader, not an improvement — despite eliminating the naive shader's "re-read all of K/V per thread" problem, per-dispatch fixed overhead on this iGPU (3 uploads + 1 download per call, many calls per UNet forward) dominates at these problem sizes. The VAE-decode blowup (19.6s→361s) is the more striking anomaly since `VaeDecoder` has no attention calls at all and wasn't touched by this change — likely GPU scheduler/memory contention from queuing many small tiled-attention dispatches ahead of it in the same process, though not root-caused; flagged here rather than assumed. Reverted immediately; shader + parity test (still passing) kept as a *correct* building block, but this is now the second consecutive real-measured failure to turn attention residency into a win on this iGPU — the attention lever looks structurally unfavorable on this specific hardware (shared-memory iGPU, small per-call payloads) rather than a tuning problem, and shouldn't be re-attempted without either much larger batched dispatches (fusing many attention calls into one) or access to a discrete GPU to re-test the premise. **Correctness re-confirmed post-mortem**: the regression run's actual output image (`docs/diffusion-samples/sdxl_tiled_attn_regression_check.png`) was visually inspected and is a correct, on-prompt apple-orchard scene matching the known-good post-CFG-fix baseline content — pure perf regression, no correctness defect |
| **Real GPU-split profiling: where the Vulkan time actually goes** | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0 (real turbo config) | Vulkan iGPU | Total 137.4s. GPU submit+exec+fence-wait: **2.7s (~2%)**. CPU-side staging-buffer Map/memcpy: **43.6s (~32%)**, across 6511 dispatches. Remaining ~91s (~65%): CPU-side work outside Vulkan entirely | 2026-09-12 | Following an external design review's own recommended first step ("measure before attempting a fix") after two failed attention-kernel attempts, added real, permanent, env-var-gated timing (`STINGRAY_PROFILE_GPU_SPLIT=1`) at the one real Vulkan choke point (`SubmitAndWait`, plus staging Map/memcpy in `Upload`/`Download`). **Real, surprising result**: the GPU itself (submit+execution+fence-wait) is NOT the bottleneck at all — only ~2% of total wall time. The dominant, measured Vulkan-attributable cost is CPU-side staging-buffer copy (~32%), from 6511 separate small Upload/Download round-trips in one 4-step run (~6.7ms average each — expensive for a memcpy, suggesting real `vkMapMemory`/cache-coherency driver overhead per call, not raw bandwidth). This directly explains why both attention-kernel attempts regressed: the kernel was being asked to overcome an architectural cost one level above it (a fixed per-call staging tax that fires regardless of kernel quality), not a compute-bound problem attention tuning could ever fix. **Real next step, not yet implemented**: collapse the UNet's per-`Lin()`/`Conv()` Upload→Dispatch→Download→Free round-trips into one whole-denoising-step-resident command-buffer recording (using `VulkanBackend`'s existing `BeginRecord`/`EndRecordAndSubmit`/`BeginBatch`/`EndBatch` batching primitives, already used elsewhere in this codebase — not new infrastructure), deferred behind measurement rather than guessed at. See `docs/00-current-work.md`'s 2026-09-12 entry for the full writeup |
| **GPU residency Stage 0+1+2: real proof-of-concept + 2 new primitives + ResBlockGpu** | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0, seed=42 | Vulkan iGPU | Total 137.4s → 118.3s (**~14% faster**). Dispatches: 6511 → 6304 (207 fewer). `stagingCopy`: 43.6s → 41.9s | 2026-09-12 | Following docs/067's staged plan (external-review-corrected): **Stage 0** proved chained, dependent GPU dispatches inside one `BeginRecord`/`EndRecordAndSubmit` session work with zero mid-graph CPU transfers (real POC test, <1e-3 diff vs. CPU reference) — confirms Sgemm's existing `DispatchOrRecord` routing already supports this, no Vulkan-backend changes needed for the compute-chaining half. **Stage 1** added two new minimal GPU primitives verified against real CPU references (`AddChannelBroadcastInPlace`: per-channel scalar broadcast into `[C,H,W]`, for ResBlock's timestep-embedding injection; `AddRowBroadcastInPlace`: per-row vector broadcast into `[N,D]`, the standard Linear-layer bias — these are NOT interchangeable, transposed broadcast axes) plus Tensor-in/Tensor-out `ConvGpuTensor`/`LinGpuTensor`/`GroupNormSiluGpuTensor` wrappers reusing existing dispatches, no new shader math beyond the two broadcast ops. **Stage 2** wired a full GPU-resident `ResBlockGpu` into `SdxlUNet2DConditionModel.ResBlock` (same design pattern as `VaeDecoder.ResBlockGpu`, adapted for the UNet's extra timestep-embedding injection step), with the same probe-once/fallback discipline. **First measurement was smaller than expected** (6511→6508 dispatches, ~8% faster) because `GroupNormSiluGpuTensor`'s weight/bias uploads were happening fresh on every call — a cost the pure-CPU `DiffusionOps.GroupNorm` path never had at all, partially offsetting the conv/Lin round-trip savings. **Caching those (invariant, small) tensors** (matching the existing `_gpuWeightsNative`/`_gpuWeights` convention) recovered the real win shown above. Output re-verified visually correct (coherent, on-prompt apple-orchard scene, matching known-good content) at the fixed seed. ResBlocks are a modest fraction of this UNet's total dispatch count (~17 blocks vs. `SpatialTransformer`'s much larger per-block Linear-projection count) — Stage 3 (`SpatialTransformer` residency) is expected to be the larger lever, not yet attempted |
| **GPU residency Stage 3a: SpatialTransformer residency, CPU attention island** | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0, seed=42 | Vulkan iGPU | Total 118.3s → 90.5s (**~23.5% faster than Stage 2, ~34% faster than the original 137.4s baseline**). Dispatches: 6304 → 4484 (1820 fewer). `stagingCopy`: 41.9s → 17.5s (**~58% drop**). Steady-state denoise step: ~19s → ~12.7s | 2026-09-12 | The larger lever Stage 2's row predicted. Required 5 new GPU primitives, all verified against real CPU references first (`SpatialTransformerGpuPrimitivesParityTests`, 7 cases): `LayerNormGpu` (per-row LayerNorm+affine, same reduction structure as `GroupNormSilu`), `GeGlu` (val*gelu(gate) gating), `PermuteChwToHwc`/`PermuteHwcToChw` (real GPU kernels for the `[C,H,W]`↔`[H*W,C]` layout conversion — written as real shaders per docs/067's explicit warning not to silently reintroduce this as a CPU round-trip), and `GroupNormGpu` (plain GroupNorm, no fused SiLU — found needed mid-implementation: `SpatialTransformer`'s pre-`proj_in` norm has no activation in the real model, unlike ResBlock's norms where the existing `GroupNormSilu` is correct; using the wrong one would have been a real, silent correctness bug caught before it shipped). `SpatialTransformerGpu` chains all of proj_in→(self-attn norm/QKV/out-proj)→(cross-attn norm/QKV/out-proj)→(FFN norm/GEGLU/proj)→proj_out GPU-resident across all transformer-block depths, with attention itself kept as an explicitly-labeled **temporary CPU-island diagnostic** (download Q/K/V → `DiffusionOps.MultiHeadAttention` → re-upload) per docs/067's Stage 3a/3b split — isolating exactly how much of this block's cost was Linear-projection round-trips (most of it, per this result) versus the attention math itself. Output re-verified pixel-identical to Stage 2's baseline at the same seed (`docs/diffusion-samples/sdxl-turbo-gpu-residency-stage3a-check.png`). Stage 3b (re-testing `MultiHeadAttentionTiled` inside this now-resident context) is the next real experiment — both its prior regressions were measured with this per-op tax still present |
| **GPU residency Stage 3b: tiled GPU attention WINS in a resident context (real reversal)** | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0, seed=42 | Vulkan iGPU | Total 90.5s → 78.6s (**~13% faster than Stage 3a, ~43% faster than the original 137.4s baseline**). Dispatches: 4484 → 2244 (**nearly halved**). `stagingCopy`: 17.5s → 8.3s. Steady-state denoise step: ~12.7s → ~9.5s | 2026-09-12 | **The tiled attention shader (`MultiHeadAttentionTiled`) now wins** — the exact same shader that regressed twice before (naive shader: 19.5s→26.8s; tiled shader: 19.5s→52-72s, see this doc's earlier rows) is a real, measured improvement here, because Q/K/V are already GPU-resident `Tensor`s from `LinGpuTensor` with zero CPU round-trip needed to call it, unlike both prior attempts where the shader was surrounded by dozens of other ops' Upload/Download tax. Implementation: `AttentionIsland` (renamed from `CpuAttentionIsland`) tries `imageOps.MultiHeadAttentionTiled` directly on the already-resident Q/K/V tensors first, with its own independent probe-once/fallback (`_residentGpuAttentionSupported`, separate from the whole-block `_spatialTransformerResidencySupported`) so a GPU-attention-specific regression would fall back to Stage 3a's CPU-island chain rather than the fully non-resident path. This is real vindication of the whole residency hypothesis: the same math, previously "a regression," is now a real win purely because the architectural tax around it was removed first — confirms neither prior attempt was a dead end on attention-kernel quality, they were measuring the wrong bottleneck |
| **GPU residency Stage 4: cross-block residency (down→mid→up chain, skip tensors resident)** | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0, seed=42 | Vulkan iGPU | Total 78.6s → 77.1s (**~2% further faster, ~44% faster than the original 137.4s baseline**). Dispatches: 2244 → 1974 (270 fewer). `stagingCopy`: 8.3s → 6.7s. **Real GPU memory measurement (new, per the plan's memory gate)**: peak device-local usage 6.67GB, 1831 live tensors at peak, well within this iGPU's ~16GB placement budget — no memory pressure at 512×512 | 2026-09-12 | Rewrote `Forward()`'s entire down→mid→up sequence as `ForwardGpu`: every `ResBlockGpu`/`SpatialTransformerGpu` call, both `CatChannels` skip-concats, both `Upsample2xGpu`+follow-up-conv pairs, and the final `GroupNormSilu`+`Conv` output stage now chain as GPU `Tensor`s with zero CPU crossings in between — only ONE `Upload` (the initial latent) and ONE `Download` (the final output) for the whole UNet forward pass. Skip-connection tensors are freed immediately after their corresponding concat consumes them (not held for the whole pass), per the plan's explicit memory discipline. **Two deliberate, bounded CPU islands remain** (labeled `StridedConvCpuIsland`): the UNet's 2 stride=2 downsample convs, since neither `Conv2dImplicitGemm` nor `ConvGpuTensor` support stride>1 — a real, scoped implementation gap, not a performance bug, and only 2 calls total per forward pass. Also added real, permanent GPU memory tracking (`STINGRAY_PROFILE_GPU_SPLIT=1`, live/peak device-local bytes + live tensor count) to `VulkanBackend`, answering the plan's Stage 4 measurement gate with real numbers instead of "checked headroom." Smaller win than Stages 2/3a/3b since most of the per-block round-trips were already eliminated by them — this stage's marginal contribution is removing the remaining inter-block boundary crossings. Output re-verified pixel-identical to Stage 3b's baseline at the same seed (`docs/diffusion-samples/sdxl-turbo-gpu-residency-stage4-check.png`) |
| ~~GPU residency Stage 5: single recording session per denoising step~~ | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0, seed=42 | Vulkan iGPU | **Real crash: `Error: [-13] ErrorUnknown - Vulkan error occured`**, on the very first denoise step — no number possible | 2026-09-12 | Attempted, real failure, reverted immediately (per this session's discipline — no silent absorption into the next stage). Wrapped `ForwardGpu` in 3 `BeginBatch()`/`EndBatch()` segments split at the 2 stride=2 downsample-conv CPU islands (`DispatchOrRecord` already auto-barriers between batched dispatches, so no manual `BatchBarrier()` needed for the compute-chaining itself). **Root cause**: `GetGpuWeight`/`GetGpuBias`/`GetNativeConvWeights` upload their GPU weight/bias tensors lazily on first cache miss — on a cold cache (every real generation's first denoise step in a fresh process), nearly every `Lin`/`Conv`/norm call inside the batched region hit a cache miss and called `Upload()`, which does its own immediate `vkQueueSubmit`+fence-wait — invalid while `_transferCmd` is still mid-recording between `BeginBatch()` and `EndBatch()`. The driver correctly rejected it. Reverted via `git checkout` back to Stage 4's committed, working state, rebuilt and re-confirmed. **Real fix path for a future attempt** (not yet implemented): pre-warm every needed GPU weight/bias tensor before the first batched segment ever starts — either a dedicated warm-up pass, or simply letting the very first `Forward()` call run unbatched (Stage 4's already-proven path) to populate every cache, then switching to Stage 5's batched path from the second `Forward()` call onward (SDXL-Turbo always calls `Forward()` multiple times per real generation, so this costs one unbatched pass total, not one per run). Current shipped state remains Stage 4 (77.1s, ~44% faster than the original 137.4s baseline) |
| ~~GPU residency Stage 5, follow-up: fixed the crash, found batching itself regresses~~ | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0, seed=42 | Vulkan iGPU | Crash fixed, but batching itself: `submitWait` 978ms→**23,899ms** (~25x), peak GPU memory 6.67GB→8.35GB, total wall time 77.1s→**79.1s** (slightly worse), despite dispatches dropping 1974→1919 | 2026-09-12 | Found the real crash cause: it wasn't cache-miss weight uploads, it was `ResBlockGpu`'s **timestep-embedding** upload — `tEmb` is real per-step activation data (not a cacheable weight), unconditionally re-uploaded on every one of ~17 `ResBlockGpu` calls, forever, so it always fired mid-batch. Fixed properly: added `UploadSiluTEmb`, uploading+SiLU-activating the shared `tEmb` ONCE per `ForwardGpu` call outside any batch, with `ResBlockGpu` taking the already-resident `Tensor`. This genuinely fixed the crash — batching then ran to completion for the first time. **But the real measurement was itself a regression**: large batched command-buffer submissions cost far more in submit/wait latency (~25x) and memory pressure (deferred frees during a big batch hold far more tensors simultaneously live) than they save in per-dispatch overhead, on this specific iGPU — a genuine negative result, not just a bug. Reverted the batching; **kept the `tEmbGpu`-once fix on its own merits** (a real simplification removing 16 redundant per-call uploads, dispatch count 1974→1910, pixel-identical output) even though its own wall-time effect measured within run-to-run noise (77-79.5s range across repeated runs) rather than a clear win. Current shipped state remains the per-op-immediate Stage 4 design (now with the `tEmbGpu` cleanup), ~44% faster than the original 137.4s baseline. Does not necessarily rule out batching on a discrete GPU with different submit-latency characteristics — flagged as hardware-specific, not assumed universal |
| **GPU residency plan (docs/067), FINAL REPORT** | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0, seed=42 | Vulkan iGPU | **137.4s → ~77-79s, a real, verified ~42-44% total wall-time reduction** — Stages 0-4 all real, measured wins; Stage 5 a real, measured negative result, reverted | 2026-09-12 | Full arc: Stage 0 proved the compute-chaining mechanism works with zero mid-graph CPU transfers; Stage 1 added the missing GPU primitives (2 broadcast-add ops, `LayerNormGpu`, `GeGlu`, CHW↔HWC permute, `GroupNormGpu`); Stage 2 made `ResBlock` GPU-resident (118.3s); Stage 3a made `SpatialTransformer` resident with a CPU attention island (90.5s); Stage 3b re-tested the previously-twice-regressed tiled attention shader now that Q/K/V are resident, and it WON (78.6s) — real vindication that neither prior attention-kernel attempt was a dead end, both were measuring the wrong bottleneck; Stage 4 chained the whole down→mid→up pass cross-block-resident (77.1s); Stage 5 (single-submission batching) was attempted, fixed a real crash, then measured as a genuine regression on this hardware and reverted. **The core finding this whole plan tested is confirmed with real numbers at every stage**: the ~137s→~77s improvement came entirely from architectural changes (removing per-op Upload/Download round-trips) with ZERO changes to any shader's actual math. **Real remaining lever, found while closing this out**: `stagingCopy`+`submitWait` are now only ~10% of total wall time — over 85% of the ~77s is CPU-side work outside Vulkan entirely (weight dequant, disk I/O, VAE decode, PNG encode), a fundamentally different investigation than this plan. See `docs/067-sdxl-unet-gpu-residency-plan.md`'s Stage 6 for the full stage-by-stage table |
| **CPU-side overhead plan (docs/068) Stage A1: real weight-read profiling finds the dominant cost, fixed same-day** | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0, seed=42 | Vulkan iGPU (CPU-side fix, backend-independent) | Total 77.0s → **67.6s (~12% faster)**, **byte-identical output hash** (no numerical drift). Weight-read: `convert` (dequant) **11.4s → 2.4s (4.7x faster)**; disk I/O unchanged at ~1.8s. Denoise step 1: 22.25s → 15.79s. Text encode: 7.63s → 5.31s (CLIP-L/CLIP-G share the same F16 read path) | 2026-09-12 | Added real, permanent, env-var-gated profiling (`STINGRAY_PROFILE_WEIGHT_READ=1`) to `SafetensorsLoader.ReadF32`, splitting it into 3 real phases (metadata lookup, disk I/O, dtype conversion) instead of treating "cold weight read" as one opaque cost — exactly the docs/068 Stage A1 gate an external review insisted on before assuming any fix. **Real, unambiguous result**: of a 13.4s total weight-read cost for this checkpoint (6.4GB, ~3.4B floats), disk I/O was only 1.8s — the dominant cost by far was `convert` at **11.4s**, a plain scalar per-element `(float)f16[i]` loop converting F16→F32, achieving only ~256M floats/s. This directly rules out weight prefetch (docs/068's C-stages) as the right next move — moving *when* a fixed 11.4s of CPU dequant work happens doesn't reduce it, and risks CPU contention with text encoding for no net gain, exactly the risk the plan's C1 gate was written to catch. Fixed with `TensorPrimitives.ConvertToSingle` (real, vectorized SIMD-widening conversion built into .NET's `System.Numerics.Tensors`, the direct mirror of `TensorPrimitives.ConvertToHalf` already used elsewhere in this codebase for the opposite direction — required adding the `System.Numerics.Tensors` package reference to `OpenTail.Stingray.Core`, which didn't have it). Verified via SHA-256 hash of the output PNG: byte-identical to the pre-fix baseline, confirming zero numerical drift, not just "looks the same." `SafetensorsLoaderHardeningTests` (16 tests) re-verified passing. This fix is backend-independent (pure CPU dequant, applies to CPU-only runs too, not just Vulkan) and applies to every safetensors-backed pipeline in this codebase, not just SDXL |
| **Same fix applied to the GGUF-loading path: `Dequantize.DequantF16`** | Real SmolLM2-1.7B GGUF inference, Vulkan iGPU | CPU dequant (backend-independent) | Same scalar-loop bug found in the GGUF dequant path (`OpenTail.Stingray.Cpu/Dequantize.cs`, shared by every F16 GGUF checkpoint's weight dequant — LLMs, vision, audio, diffusion, not just safetensors-loaded models). Fixed identically with `TensorPrimitives.ConvertToSingle` | 2026-09-12 | User asked directly whether the safetensors F16-dequant fix generalizes — checked, and yes: `GgufWeightLoader.ReadF32` delegates to `Dequantize.ToFloat32`, whose `DequantF16` case was the exact same `(float)halves[i]` scalar loop. Fixed the same way (`System.Numerics.Tensors` package reference added to `OpenTail.Stingray.Cpu`, which didn't have it). Verified with a real end-to-end GGUF inference run (SmolLM2-1.7B-Instruct Q4_K_M, Vulkan): correct, coherent output ("What is the capital of France?" → "The capital of France is Paris."), not just a build check. `Dequantize.DequantBF16` (also scalar, GGUF's BF16 case) was left as-is — .NET's `TensorPrimitives` has no native BFloat16 widening conversion, so this would need a different (SIMD bit-shift) approach; flagged as a further opportunity, not fixed this pass. This fix's real blast radius is large: `Dequantize.ToFloat32` is the shared dequant entry point for every GGUF-loaded model in this entire codebase whenever a checkpoint stores any tensor (commonly embeddings/norms even in otherwise-quantized checkpoints) as F16 |
| **CPU-side overhead plan (docs/068) Stages B1-B4: full VAE decoder GPU residency (mid-block through conv_out)** | 512×512, 4 steps, guidance=0, seed=42 | Vulkan iGPU | VAE decode: **20.42s → 12.23s (~40% faster)**; total pipeline: ~69.5s → 61.6s | 2026-09-12 | Extended `VaeDecoder` with `MidBlockAndFirstUpBlockGpu`/`UpBlockGpu`/`DecodeRestGpu`, making the whole mid-block-through-`conv_out` sequence fully GPU-resident (only one Upload of the input latent and one Download of the final tensor cross the CPU boundary) — reusing 100% pre-existing GPU primitives (`ResBlockGpu`, `GroupNormSiluTensor`, `ConvNativeTensor`, `Upsample2xGpu`, `AddInPlace`), no new shader math. The mid-block's single attention call stays a deliberate CPU island (see the B5 finding: real per-substage profiling showed VAE attention is <5% of decode time, so a GPU port there was correctly skipped rather than assumed worthwhile). First small cut (mid-block only, B1) measured a marginal 20.42s→19.81s — confirmed the pattern was numerically correct (byte-identical hash) before extending to the full decoder in one combined B2-B4 pass, which delivered the real win. **Verification**: output PNG visually inspected — same coherent apple/orchard scene, no corruption. The resulting hash (`6f340ff6...`) differs from the established baseline (`5a738c6f...`); rather than assume this is benign, root-caused it directly — added a temporary `STINGRAY_VAE_FORCE_CPU=1` gate, re-ran the identical seed/prompt through the untouched CPU-orchestrated fallback path in isolation, and got back the exact baseline hash. Confirms the difference is floating-point non-associativity from GPU op reordering (same benign-divergence class seen throughout docs/067's UNet residency work), not a correctness regression. The temporary gate was removed after verification; not part of shipped code |
| **SDXL SpatialTransformer Attention Microbenchmarking & Optimization (docs/085)** | Isolated attention kernel microbenchmarks (6 SDXL shapes, 7 compute models) | Vulkan iGPU vs CPU (AVX2/FMA) | 4096×4096 L1 Self-Attn: **345.7ms (FP32) → 150.7ms (FP16)** (**2.29× faster**, 3.40× vs CPU); 1024×1024: **43.8ms → 18.8ms** (**2.33× faster**) | 2026-09-17 | Comprehensive attention kernel microbenchmarking audit (`SdxlAttentionMicrobenchmarkTests`) assessing 7 models across all 6 SDXL UNet shapes (`hw=4096, 1024, 256` self/cross). **Key findings & fixes**: (1) *Workgroup Dispatch Bug Fixed*: `VulkanBackend.cs:3971` was dispatching `(qSeq + 15) / 16` workgroups instead of `(qSeq + 31) / 32` for $BR=32$ vectorized kernels, truncating query evaluations by half; (2) *Vega 8 iGPU Bandwidth Bottleneck Identified*: Shared-memory APU bus saturates FP32 tiled attention at 115–124 GFLOP/s; (3) *FP16 Fused Tiled Attention Breakthrough (`MultiHeadAttentionTiled64_FP16`)*: Using $BR=32, BC=16, WG=128$, `f16vec4` memory loads, and FP32 register accumulation cuts global DRAM traffic in half, lifting compute density to **250–286 GFLOP/s** across all shapes with machine-precision parity (maxDiff < 8.61e-6 vs CPU reference). Self-attention runtime dropped to **150.7 ms** at $4096\times 4096$ and **18.8 ms** at $1024\times 1024$; (4) *Discrete GEMM vs Fused Attention Trade-off*: Discrete GEMM (`ScoreGemm` + `Softmax` + `ValueGemm`) runs faster at small token counts ($256\times 256$: 2.39ms vs 2.83ms FP32 fused), but materializing score matrices consumes up to 671 MB scratch VRAM at $4096\times 4096$, proving fused online-softmax attention is non-negotiable for resident UNet graphs. |

**SDXL UNet SpatialTransformer Attention Kernel Scaling — Performance Check: 2026-09-17 (`SdxlAttentionMicrobenchmarkTests`, docs/085):**

| Attention Shape ($Q \times K$) | Description | AVX2 CPU SIMD | FP32 Tiled GPU | FP16 Fused Tiled GPU (`MultiHeadAttentionTiled64_FP16`) | Kernel Speedup (FP16 vs FP32) |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|:---:|
| **$4096 \times 4096$** | Level 1 Self-Attention | 512.7 ms | 345.7 ms | **150.7 ms** | **2.29× faster** (3.40× vs CPU) |
| **$4096 \times 77$** | Level 1 Cross-Attention | 8.8 ms | 15.6 ms | **6.7 ms** | **2.33× faster** (1.31× vs CPU) |
| **$1024 \times 1024$** | Level 2 Self-Attention | 63.6 ms | 43.8 ms | **18.8 ms** | **2.33× faster** (3.38× vs CPU) |
| **$1024 \times 77$** | Level 2 Cross-Attention | 2.5 ms | 4.3 ms | **2.2 ms** | **1.95× faster** (1.14× vs CPU) |
| **$256 \times 256$** | Level 3 Self-Attention | 4.4 ms | 2.8 ms | **1.5 ms** | **1.87× faster** (2.93× vs CPU) |
| **$256 \times 77$** | Level 3 Cross-Attention | 0.8 ms | 1.1 ms | **0.8 ms** | **1.38× faster** (Parity with CPU) |

> No C++ reference attempted for either (no vendored image/video-diffusion C++ CLI in this repo).
> `hunyuanvideo` and `ltx-t5` were not attempted — no wired end-to-end CLI/test path was found for
> either, and given Wan2.1's real ~71-minute cost for just 2 frames, both would likely take
> considerably longer still.

## CUDA Inference — No Numbers Yet

> **No CUDA GPU on dev machine.** No measured numbers exist for CUDA on this box.
> See `GR_performance.md` and `docs/done/gpu-review-log.md` for the code-level audit.
> All CUDA rows above are from prior hardware (†).
>
> **Highest-confidence open opportunity:** Q6_K and Q5_K prefill currently dequantize full weight
> matrix to FP16 then call cuBLAS — a full-weight HBM round-trip per call. Direct MMQ (as llama.cpp
> does) would eliminate this. Requires real NVIDIA hardware to validate.

## Known Measurement Gaps & C++ Reference Head-to-Head

> **Purpose:** Tracking performance parity vs authoritative C++ reference implementations (`sd-cli.exe`, `audiocpp_cli.exe`, `llama.cpp`).
> The table below reports both **End-to-End** pipeline execution times and **Granular Stage Breakdowns** (Denoise loop, Text conditioning, VAE decode) on separate rows to pinpoint exactly **"how much are we out by?"** (Ratio column) and **"where"** the bottlenecks lie.

| **LTX-Video-2B — seed sweep, `--cfg-scale 6.0`** | **End-to-End (512×512, 1 frame, 20 steps, seeds 55 & 100)** | Vulkan iGPU | **303.3s (seed 100) / 308.9s (seed 55)** | *sd-cli blocked on audio cross-attn* | consistent w/ CFG=3.0 timing | 2026-09-18 | **CLOSES the item to 🟢.** Both seeds had produced abstract/messy (not noise, but not coherent) output at the default `--cfg-scale 3.0`; re-running at 6.0 produced clean, coherent, clearly recognizable red-apple-on-wood-table images for both (`docs/diffusion-samples/ltx_video_apple_vulkan_seed55_cfg6_2026-09-18.png`, `..._seed100_cfg6_2026-09-18.png`). 4 of 4 tested seeds (42, 7, 55, 100) now coherent. Timing unaffected by CFG value -- no cost to the fix. Root-caused as guidance-strength sensitivity, not a structural bug: this checkpoint's own upstream repo documents needing per-prompt CFG/step tuning. |
| **LTX-Video-2B — re-verification, seed 42, `--cfg-scale 6.0`, 2026-09-19** | **End-to-End (512×512, 1 frame, 20 steps, seed 42)** | Vulkan iGPU | **472.8s** | *sd-cli blocked on audio cross-attn* | vs. 303.3-308.9s (seeds 55/100, same CFG, 2026-09-18) | 2026-09-19 | Output confirmed coherent (recognizable red apple on a wooden table with other objects, abstract/painterly, matching this checkpoint's documented style). **Real, honest discrepancy: this run is ~1.5-1.56x SLOWER than the seed 55/100 runs one day earlier, at the identical config** (512×512/20-step/cfg=6.0). Not explained by seed (timing shouldn't meaningfully depend on which seed is used) or CFG (already confirmed CFG-independent in the row above). Real candidate causes not ruled out this pass: (a) background resource contention during this specific run wasn't checked *beforehand* this time (only checked after, finding the machine idle by then -- inconclusive for what was happening during the run itself); (b) a real regression from unrelated infra changes landed between 2026-09-18 and 2026-09-19 (this session touched shared Vulkan files -- `VulkanBackend.cs`/`Shaders.cs`/`Shaders.Precompiled.g.cs` -- for FLUX.2's new `SiluGateMul` shader, though that's an unrelated compute pipeline and shouldn't affect LTX-Video's own dispatch pipelines). **Real bisection done, same day**: re-ran seed 100 directly (documented at 303.3s on 2026-09-18) -- got **460.9s today, confirming a real, consistent ~1.5x slowdown, not seed-specific noise.** Investigated the leading candidate (b) directly: diffed the compiled SPIR-V bytecode in `Shaders.Precompiled.g.cs` between the pre-FLUX2-work commit (`798a046`) and the current one -- found the `SgemmF16` shader's compiled bytecode blob genuinely differs (different MD5) despite identical GLSL source (same content hash), from `scripts/gen-spirv.ps1` being re-run for FLUX.2's new `SiluGateMul` shader. **Tested this directly**: temporarily swapped the OLD precompiled table into the working tree, rebuilt, re-ran seed 100 -- got **463.0s, essentially unchanged from 460.9s with the new table. This DISPROVES the shader-regen hypothesis** -- ruled out by direct empirical test, not just reasoning. Restored the correct (new) table immediately after and rebuilt to confirm the repo is back to a clean, correct state. **Real conclusion**: both of today's runs (460.9s seed 100, 472.8s seed 42) are internally consistent with each other but both differ from the prior day's number -- the most parsimonious explanation is thermal/power throttling on this shared-memory iGPU after several hours of sustained heavy real-weight GPU/CPU workload earlier in today's session (multiple FLUX.2 GPU tests, SDXL-Turbo CPU+GPU runs, this LTX-Video investigation itself), not a code regression. **Not fully proven** (would need a fresh-boot re-run to confirm thermal throttling specifically vs. some other environmental factor), but the shader-bytecode candidate is definitively ruled out, and no source-code change in this session touches LTX-Video's own dispatch path. |

| **FLUX.2-dev (Q4_K_S)** | **End-to-End (128×128, 4 steps, seed 42)** | CPU | **97.8s**<br>*(was 300.8s)* | *PyTorch CPU unrunnable (OOM)* | **3.1x faster vs pre-opt** | 2026-09-18 | **GATING CONDITION 1 MET & 3.1x SPEEDUP**: Pre-transposed `QuantizedWeightCache` + structural token-order fixes. Output verified photorealistic apple on wooden table (`docs/diffusion-samples/flux2_quality_check_128_4step_2026-09-18.png`), completely eliminating the checkerboard/grid artifact. Diagnosed and fixed 3 compounding structural bugs: (1) `InterleavedRoPE` adjacent-pair rotation `(2*d, 2*d + 1)` instead of half-split; (2) `ApplyDoubleBlockReal` row-wise QKV unpack instead of flat striding; (3) `LastLayer` AdaLN modulation and affine-free `LayerNormNoAffine`. Reference comparison: PyTorch CPU (`examples/flux2`) requires ~120 GB RAM in FP32 (crashes with OOM on 64GB machine); PyTorch Zen 3 CPU lacks AVX-512 BF16 and falls back to scalar software emulation. Stingray fits entirely in 17.37 GB RAM. |

If a large C++-vs-C# gap this size (10-20x) is real, it likely points at the same architectural issue already diagnosed for SDXL/Vulkan (per-dispatch Upload/Sgemm/Synchronize round-trips vs. audio.cpp's ahead-of-time static `ggml_gallocr` graph — see `docs/00-current-work.md`'s 2026-09-12 Vulkan profiling entry) rather than a raw kernel-speed gap. |
